14:I[8669,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DateSidebar"]
15:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
16:T504,The proposed MOSAIC framework formulates MoE architecture-systems co-design as a mixed-integer nonlinear program. First, the authors derive a MoE scaling law that demonstrates the necessity of systems constraints: under model-FLOPs-only constraints, optimal MoE sparsity sits at the feasibility boundary, with loss monotonically decreasing as sparsity increases, meaning no interior optimum exists without explicit systems limits. Second, a calibrated system performance model estimates, for any given MoE architecture on a target cluster, attainable MFU, communication overhead across data/tensor/pipeline/expert parallelism dimensions, memory usage, and the best available parallel layout. Third, instead of solving a continuous unconstrained optimum and snapping to the nearest valid discrete architecture (the standard practice), MOSAIC directly minimizes predicted loss over a discrete grid of valid architecture choices—expert count, active parameters, total parameters, training token count, layer dimensions—to jointly output a cluster-tailored MoE geometry, token budget, and parallel execution plan. Unlike prior work that added only memory constraints to MoE scaling laws, MOSAIC accounts for the full stack of systems overheads that determine real training throughput.17:T4b5,The analysis starts by decomposing each FFN expert into hidden channels. Each channel has an up-projection key, which controls activation, and a down-projection value, which determines the output direction. In a sparsely upcycled MoE, experts are initialized from the same pretrained FFN, so the same channel index remains comparable across experts. The authors use this to measure whether co-activated experts write similar value directions at aligned positions. They find that co-activated experts are partially aligned rather than fully redundant; removing aligned positions changes the preferred experts; and tokens with larger shared coverage need fewer residual experts to reconstruct the original output. UniF-MoE implements this principle with one shared expert and K residual experts per layer, all divided into aligned blocks. A token-level router first estimates shared demand, then selects shared blocks through key prototypes, routes only complementary blocks to residual experts, and uses cumulative routing mass to decide how many residual experts to activate. A Gram constraint shapes router embeddings into normalized and distinct directions, encouraging specialization with less overlap.18:T403,Continuous Interaction Diffusion (CID) separates state into three coupled channels: a fact channel containing externally controlled, read-only information; a thought channel represented as a Typed Cognitive Tensor (TCT), a continuous locally diffused structure with role distributions, symbolic anchors, source links, uncertainty, and per-cell noise levels; and a display channel, the discrete token canvas visible to the user. The key abstraction is a persistent perceptual binding: when the model still needs an external fact, the runtime maintains a binding between that information need and its source. Static sources can be cached and repeatedly re-projected into the evolving thought state; changing sources can be refreshed or streamed asynchronously. This avoids re-serializing identical tool calls merely to keep information cognitively active. The paper also specifies an asynchronous runtime, local diffusion clocks, a model-side intent interface, training objectives, and an evaluation protocol for read-only tools.19:T47e,Three empirical findings are central. First, after pretraining, the deductive outputs are reported to be nearly invariant to input on tested workloads, with relative fluctuations around 10^-6 to 10^-11 and zero at floating-point resolution for the strongest cases. Replacing the nonlinear PLGA subnetwork by a cached G_LM leaves the published cached-versus-uncached benchmark scores unchanged, with additional operator-level and logit-level agreement reported in Appendix D. Second, the generator A is numerically rank one under an explicit singular-value tolerance, with identical rows and identical rows across heads within a layer. A_LM remains strictly positive and has Perron-Frobenius structure, while being near full numerical rank; determinant zeros are attributed to underflow rather than true low rank. Third, the released experiments associate generalization with a critical-like training regime controlled by maximum learning rate and warmup steps, using deductive-output fluctuations as an order parameter. The paper does not provide a proof that operator invariance must occur, nor a controlled claim of performance advantage over SDPA.1a:T4fc,The method, SHIM, adds an offline-trained logit correction on top of standard masked decoding. The correction is conditioned on parser state, lexer state, and the candidate next token. The central observation is that incremental parsing infrastructure already tracks where the current prefix sits in both grammar space and lexical space. Parser state alone is too coarse when the model is partway through a lexeme; lexer state captures character-level progress inside an unfinished tokenizable unit. The paper’s BV4 example makes this concrete: several operator completions may still be possible while the parser remains unchanged, but the lexer has already narrowed the continuation set substantially. SHIM learns a multiplicative adjustment factor that approximates how likely a candidate token is to admit a valid continuation in the future. At inference time, this becomes an additive correction in logit space. Compared with online sampling methods, SHIM gives up exact recovery in exchange for low constant overhead. Compared with surrogate probabilistic models, it does not try to model the full LM distribution; it only learns the missing global reachability signal that local masking fails to encode. That narrower target is why the controller can stay lightweight.1b:T43f,From the available full-text excerpt, the experimental story appears to have three main components. First, compare against plain masking-based GCD to show better fidelity and output quality under the same hard grammatical guarantees. Second, compare against online resampling approaches to show a better latency-quality tradeoff. Third, run ablations over parser-only, lexer-only, and combined state features to demonstrate that lexer state is essential when validity depends on progress inside an unfinished lexeme. The introduction clearly claims that SHIM better matches the LM’s distribution over valid sequences and avoids collapse toward low-probability but locally admissible outputs. For a careful read, the numbers worth checking are the controller size, how training examples are constructed from parser transitions and valid token sequences, per-step latency overhead, task-level gains on structured generation benchmarks, and wall-clock comparisons against online sampling. The excerpt provided here does not include the full tables, so I cannot state exact gains reliably.1c:T436,CurveFP is a block-scaled closed-product codebook family: 1) Quantized magnitudes are distributed across K interleaved logarithmic curves; each nonzero element stores a sign, two's-complement exponent, and curve index under a shared per-block power-of-two scale. 2) A rational radix r=2^(p/q) parameterizes the logarithmic lattice: p/q<1 contracts range and improves local resolution, while p/q>1 expands range. 3) Uniform curve spacing makes multiplication exactly closed in the code domain: sign XOR, curve-index addition, and exponent addition with carry — no lookup table, projection, or general multiplier is needed to form the product code. 4) A finite phase count qK/gcd(p,qK) is derived to explicitly map representation choices to the number of fractional-exponent classes that accumulation must combine, yielding an explicit quality-vs-complexity contract. 5) One algebra is instantiated as CurveFP8 for training (E4C3 for weights/activations, E5C2 for gradients, mirroring the range split of FP8 E4M3/E5M2) and CurveFP7 (E3C3, 12.5% narrower than FP8) for inference.1d:T78e,The core contribution is a corrected protocol for measuring cross-lingual action-policy retention. Under a fixed scaffold, models operate over a shared five-tool alphabet and emit executed action traces alongside answers. The study covers 8 models, 6 parallel benchmarks, 41 languages, and 2.38 million rollouts. A naive trace-similarity metric fails for five reasons: short traces look artificially similar, empty traces score perfectly, unrelated traces overlap by chance, same-language reruns are not identical, and without a within-language baseline the quantity is not identifiable.

The estimator addresses each issue explicitly. Every language-task cell is run twice with identical decoding settings and token budgets, changing only the serving seed. Same-language agreement, I_within, compares the two replicates in one language. Cross-language agreement, I_cross, compares traces across languages, also cross-seed. The normalized retention metric is \tilde{I}=I_cross/I_within, which measures how much of a model’s own reproducible policy survives a language change. Comparisons are length-matched in both directions, pairs are dropped if either trace is empty, and uncertainty is estimated with task-level bootstraps. This design makes language the only systematic difference while keeping decoding noise symmetric.

The paper then probes mechanism. The evidence points to an English pivot. Translate is the most frequently used tool across adapted benchmarks, and reasoning text is about 99% ASCII even for non-Latin inputs such as Devanagari. Removing the translation tool lowers agreement in proportion to a model’s prior reliance on translation; forcing translation helps in a pre-registered ordering across models. The implication is that many multilingual agents do not execute the same policy directly in the target language. They often map the task into an English-centered internal workflow and plan from there.1e:T697,The empirical scale is substantial: 8 models, 6 parallel benchmarks, 41 languages, and 2.38M rollouts. Three results stand out. First, under greedy decoding, all 24 main cells show a positive cross-lingual policy gap, and every bootstrap interval on the raw gap excludes zero. The effect is stable rather than anecdotal. Second, every correction increases the measured effect instead of shrinking it. That is important because it means the problem was previously hidden by bad measurement, not because it was weak. Third, after normalization, four otherwise very different models converge to nearly the same policy-retention value under greedy decoding: about 71–73%. The regularity holds at the cell level, not just in model averages, and model identity explains only about 5.7% of the variance.

The temperature ladder is also informative. As sampling temperature rises, cross-language agreement changes little, while same-language self-consistency drops sharply. Without normalization, sampling noise masks the true cross-lingual policy loss. On the mechanism side, Translate is the most-used tool in every adapted benchmark, and reasoning text is almost entirely ASCII, indicating that non-English tasks are often routed through an English internal channel. Removing translation hurts agreement roughly in proportion to how much a model already uses it; forcing translation helps in the pre-registered order predicted from those usage rates. When reading the full paper, it is worth checking how tightly the tool alphabet is standardized across benchmarks, how the bidirectional length matching is implemented, and whether retention differs systematically across language families.1f:T50e,This one is worth a careful read. Start with the introduction and method sections to understand why the paper insists on measuring action traces rather than final answers, and how each of the five confounds can distort the conclusion. Then read the section defining I_within, I_cross, and \tilde{I}; that is the identification core of the paper. After that, inspect the main result figures, especially the claim that every correction makes the effect larger. Finally, read the temperature analysis and the translation-tool ablations, which explain why multilingual agents can look answer-consistent while still following different internal policies.

From a pretraining angle, two questions are especially worth carrying through the paper. First, is the English-pivot behavior driven mainly by pretraining data distribution, tokenizer effects, or English-heavy tool-use post-training data? Second, does the near-constant 71–73% retention hint at a structural bottleneck in cross-lingual policy transfer, where only part of the planner survives language change? A full read of the main text plus the key appendices will likely take 45–60 minutes. The appendix sections on the estimator, confound costs, and design checks are essential; skipping them makes the result look simpler than it is.20:T461,VERDICT formulates verification as a coupled scoring problem among frozen, modality-specialized judges.
- Three frozen judges score each candidate step from different perspectives: Visual grounding, Logical validity, and Contextual relevance.
- Disagreement-aware consensus: instead of aggregating independently, they introduce coupling and interpret the interaction as a coordination game. The consensus-adjusted scores correspond to a unique Nash equilibrium with a claimed closed-form solution, so each judge’s adjusted belief accounts for others.
- Dual acceptance rule: a mean-confidence threshold ensures sufficient collective endorsement, while a dispersion (agreement) constraint rejects unstable steps with unresolved conflict. Among accepted candidates, they rank by consensus confidence to pick the most stable continuation.
- Key distinction vs mean/variance: averaging is symmetric and loses “who disagrees with whom”; variance ignores belief strength. Coupling introduces structured, asymmetric influence (e.g., a stubborn visual judge can dominate disagreement in a way fixed weights cannot capture).21:T4be,The method is called C3. For each task instance, the authors generate multiple context perturbations that preserve the task semantics while changing the surrounding framing. They then compare the model’s output distribution under the original prompt and under the perturbed prompts. The magnitude of that distributional shift becomes the instance-level C3 score: smaller shifts indicate higher credibility. A notable strength is that C3 is not tied to one output format. The paper adapts the comparison procedure across multiple-choice, short-answer, long-form factuality, and code generation settings, using task-appropriate estimators of output-distribution change. The motivation section uses a logical-semantics framing: if a model stably supports a proposition, semantically irrelevant contextual changes should not move its induced answer distribution much. This is not a mechanistic theory of LLM internals, but it is a coherent behavioral rationale. Relative to self-consistency, C3 measures stability across prompts rather than repeated samples from one prompt. Relative to paraphrase consistency, it tries to avoid changing the question itself, reducing false inconsistency caused by altered semantics.22:T518,The evaluation spans 26 models and 6 benchmarks, with 16 models in the main analysis and an additional 10-model case study. The task mix includes reasoning, factuality, and code generation. The main empirical findings are threefold. At the instance level, smaller cross-contextual shifts are associated with higher correctness or factuality. At the model level, aggregated C3 scores produce a robustness profile that distinguishes models that are merely benchmark-competent from those that remain behaviorally stable under semantically neutral perturbations. At the method level, the signal appears robust across perturbation sources and across different estimators of distributional difference. The paper also argues that C3 remains informative when standard benchmarks are less discriminative because of saturation or contamination, since it does not rely directly on answer-key scoring. For a closer read, the key numbers to inspect are the correlation between C3 and accuracy/factuality on each task, whether the effect holds for both open and closed models, which error types show the largest shifts, and how C3 compares with self-consistency, paraphrase consistency, and calibration-style baselines. The excerpt provided here does not include the full tables, so I cannot quote exact values safely.23:T8cc,文章先做了两个诊断，再提出 SALT。第一个诊断是证明动作轨迹确实含有超出视觉结果的 verb 信息。作者把 verb grounding 拆成两部分：action goals，对应 episode 起止状态的视觉变化；motion dynamics，对应 7-DoF 连续动作轨迹。通过信息分析，他们发现两者各自都携带对方无法完全替代的 verb 信息，也就是说，单看图像前后状态不足以恢复动作语义，动作过程本身包含语言相关结构。

第二个诊断是现有离散动作表示会损伤这种结构。作者比较了 Bin tokenization、VQ-VAE tokenizer 和 FAST 等方案，考察 tokenized action representation 与 verb 之间的 mutual information。结论是：只按重建训练的 tokenizer 会系统性降低 verb 相关信息，而且压缩越强，损失越大。更重要的是，后续语言条件 policy training 不能完全把这部分结构补回来，说明瓶颈发生在 tokenizer 这一步，而不是 policy head 太弱。

在此基础上，作者提出 SALT，Semantically ALigned action Tokenizer。它以 VQ-VAE 风格 tokenizer 为基础，但在 tokenizer 训练阶段加入一个语言对齐辅助目标：把量化后的 action latent 输入一个冻结的预训练 VLM，让它生成对应 episode instruction。这样做的关键不是让 tokenizer 直接分类 verb，而是通过 instruction generation 这个更丰富的监督，把“被相似语言描述的动作”拉到更接近的离散表示，同时保留连续控制空间的重建能力。训练目标因此是 reconstruction / commitment / codebook loss 与 instruction-generation loss 的联合优化。这里选择冻结 VLM，而不是端到端共同训练，目的是把语言语义作为稳定教师信号注入 tokenizer 学习，避免 policy 训练阶段再去间接恢复。

从建模角度看，SALT 的重要区别在于：它把 action tokenizer 从单纯的压缩模块变成了跨模态语义瓶颈。相比 per-dimension binning，SALT 学的是组合式离散词表；相比 reconstruction-only VQ-VAE，它显式约束 codebook 保留语言可恢复性；相比 FAST 这类频域压缩+BPE，它不只追求序列压缩率，而是优先保留对 instruction 有用的结构。24:T962,The paper first establishes two empirical diagnoses, then introduces SALT. The first diagnosis is that action trajectories contain verb information beyond visual state changes. The authors decompose verb grounding into action goals, represented by visual changes between episode endpoints, and motion dynamics, represented by the 7-DoF action trajectory. Their analysis shows that each carries verb-relevant information not fully captured by the other. This means that visual outcomes alone are insufficient; the action process itself contains language-relevant structure.

The second diagnosis is that existing discrete action representations erase this structure. The paper compares Bin tokenization, VQ-VAE tokenizers, and FAST, and measures the mutual information between verbs and tokenized action representations. Reconstruction-only tokenizers consistently reduce verb-related information, with stronger compression causing larger losses. Importantly, downstream language-conditioned policy training does not fully recover the missing structure, indicating that the bottleneck is created at the tokenizer interface rather than by an underpowered policy head.

SALT—Semantically ALigned action Tokenizer—is then built on top of a VQ-VAE-style tokenizer. During tokenizer training, the quantized action latents are fed into a frozen pretrained VLM, which is asked to generate the corresponding episode instruction. This adds a language-alignment objective to the usual reconstruction, commitment, and codebook losses. The point is not to classify verbs explicitly, but to force the codebook to preserve information that makes the original instruction recoverable. Actions described by similar language are therefore encouraged to occupy nearby or related discrete representations while maintaining control-space fidelity.

The key modeling difference is that SALT treats the tokenizer as a cross-modal bottleneck. Compared with per-dimension binning, it learns a compositional discrete vocabulary. Compared with reconstruction-only VQ-VAE, it explicitly constrains the codebook to retain language-recoverable structure. Compared with FAST-style frequency compression plus BPE, it prioritizes semantic preservation over compression alone. Using a frozen VLM as the teacher also stabilizes the language signal and avoids relying on downstream policy training to rediscover the missing semantics.25:T6f8,The experiments have three parts. First, on BridgeV2, the paper analyzes where verb information comes from and shows that both visual endpoint changes and action trajectories contribute unique information. That supports the premise that action representations need their own language alignment rather than inheriting everything from vision-language pretraining.

Second, the paper studies information loss under tokenization. Across Bin, VQ-VAE, and FAST, mutual information between verbs and tokenized action representations drops, and the drop becomes larger under stronger compression. This is the core evidence that the discrete action interface is a semantic bottleneck.

Third, the paper evaluates downstream control in SimplerEnv. Policies trained with SALT reach 71.9% average task success, compared with 42.7% for a reconstruction-only VQ-VAE tokenizer and 31.2% for FAST. The gap is large enough to show that semantic alignment is not merely improving interpretability; it materially affects closed-loop control performance.

The abstract also notes that although the auxiliary objective does not explicitly label verbs, the learned action vocabulary becomes organized around verb-relevant distinctions. In the full paper, the most informative evidence here is likely to be codebook visualizations, nearest-neighbor analyses, instruction recoverability, or token-cluster semantics. Those qualitative analyses matter because they reveal whether SALT is truly learning language-aligned action primitives rather than simply benefiting from extra supervision. The most useful ablations to inspect are the weight of the instruction-generation loss, codebook size, compression level, the choice of frozen VLM, and whether policy comparisons are capacity-matched across tokenizers.26:T473,This is a good skim paper, but the logic matters. Start with Sections 2 and 3, because the method only makes sense if two premises hold: trajectories contain verb information beyond visual outcomes, and standard tokenizers destroy part of it. Then read the SALT method section and the training objective carefully. The key question is why the semantic supervision is applied during tokenizer learning rather than deferred to policy learning, and why instruction generation from quantized latents is an effective way to preserve language-recoverable structure. Finish with the SimplerEnv results and the codebook or token-semantic analyses.

A useful first pass should take about 35–50 minutes. The figures worth focusing on are the verb-information decomposition, mutual information versus compression, the main downstream success-rate table, and any visualization of token clusters or codebook organization. If your interest is unified multimodal pretraining, it is worth mentally replacing action tokens with image or audio tokens while reading; the same failure mode likely exists there as well, but action makes it easier to observe.27:T74f,The method has two main components. The first is a heuristic poisoning strategy. Instead of teaching the model a fixed trigger-target lookup table, the poisoned data is designed to make the model treat the trigger as a semantic control signal. In effect, the model is encouraged to prioritize information carried by the trigger when the trigger is present, rather than memorizing one malicious caption per trigger. The paper explicitly frames this as exploiting the VLM's native zero-shot generalization: the model is pushed to generalize from seen poisoned patterns to unseen target-caption semantics at inference time.

The second component is trigger steganography, denoted TS(·). Given an arbitrary target caption z, TS maps z into a stealthy visual trigger, either as norm-controlled noise or as a semantically meaningless patch. The key idea is dynamic trigger synthesis: the trigger is generated from the desired target text at inference time, rather than being fixed during poisoning. The excerpt suggests that this mapping is built around the representation of a vanilla trigger in the visual feature space. That is, the attack first identifies a trigger representation that reliably activates the backdoor, then embeds target-text information into that representation while keeping the perturbation visually subtle.

Conceptually, this is the main departure from one-to-one and N-to-N backdoors. In standard settings, the target set is fixed during poisoning. Here, target selection is decoupled from poisoning and moved to inference time. The hard part is making the model generalize to unseen target semantics while keeping the trigger stealthy and preserving benign utility. The paper also claims robustness against classical backdoor defenses, which suggests the attack is not relying on an obviously visible patch or a trivially separable poisoned subset.28:T758,From the provided full content, the experiments are conducted on LLaVA with Flickr8k and Flickr30k. The evaluation appears to cover two broad aspects. One is benign utility: whether the backdoored model retains normal captioning or multimodal understanding performance on clean inputs. The other is attack efficacy: whether dynamically generated triggers can induce the model to output attacker-specified captions whose semantics were not pre-bound during poisoning. The paper emphasizes that its any-to-any caption-control setting is more flexible than fixed-mapping backdoors and demonstrates control over previously unseen target-caption semantics.

The excerpt does not include the full numeric tables, but the likely key metrics are clear. First, there should be an attack success measure, such as exact target match, semantic similarity to the desired caption, or a captioning metric against the attacker-specified text. Second, the paper likely compares different trigger forms, such as noise-based versus patch-based triggers, to show the trade-off between stealth and controllability. Third, it should compare against one-to-one or N-to-N baselines to show that programmability is not achieved only by sacrificing clean performance. Fourth, since robustness to classical defenses is claimed, the defense section is important: it matters whether the tested defenses are data filtering, activation clustering, fine-tuning-based cleansing, or input-transformation methods.

When reading the full paper, the most informative numbers to extract are the poisoning ratio, the exact LLaVA variant and scale, whether the target captions are fully out of the poisoning target set, the drop in clean caption quality, the success rate of dynamic target control, the performance-stealth trade-off under different perturbation budgets, and the attack success before and after defenses.29:T5b7,This is a skim paper. Start with the introduction and threat model to understand the shift from fixed-mapping backdoors to programmable any-to-any control. Then read the method overview figure to separate the roles of heuristic poisoning and TS(·). After that, go straight to the experiments and focus on three result groups: clean utility, dynamic target control, and robustness to defenses. If you are reading selectively, 20 to 30 minutes is enough to capture the main contribution.

Two questions are worth keeping in mind while reading. First, does the model truly learn to decode target semantics from the trigger, or is it exploiting a weaker feature-matching shortcut that only looks programmable under the tested setup? That determines how scalable the threat really is. Second, how much access to the victim model is required to synthesize triggers at inference time? If the method depends heavily on gradients or internal feature access, the real-world attack surface is narrower than the headline suggests. The appendix is likely important for notation, poisoned data construction, and defense settings, since those details often determine how realistic the attack is.

For pretraining researchers, the main takeaway is not the attack recipe itself but the evaluation lesson: multimodal semantic generalization can amplify the impact of poisoned data, so security testing should move beyond fixed triggers and include open-ended programmable control.2a:T628,TideRL is an elastic asynchronous distributed system built around a shared global readiness view, with three core co-designed components: 1) Continuous Task Batching (CTB), which replaces request-level continuous batching on rollout ranks with task-level admission, preemption, and resumption. It tracks per-rank token memory footprints in real time, uses semaphores to control KV cache preemption priority, and actively pins KV caches for multi-turn tasks expected to resume soon, eliminating redundant prefill overhead from cache eviction during idle periods; 2) Resource-Aware Ref-Actor Pipelining (RA²P), which abandons fixed ref-actor execution modes on training nodes and dynamically switches between two modes based on current ready backlog size and data arrival interval: decoupled streaming mode places ref and actor on separate GPUs for large backlogs and fast arrival to avoid weight swapping thrashing, while colocated aggregation mode lets ref and actor share GPU memory for small backlogs and slow arrival to reduce cross-node communication. It also overlaps ref inference and actor gradient computation at the micro-batch level to eliminate idle bubbles; 3) Elastic Resource Scaling (ERS), which treats individual data parallel ranks as schedulable resources instead of statically assigning them to rollout or training groups. The system monitors the source of current goodput loss in real time (insufficient rollout throughput or backlogged training data) and dynamically migrates GPU ranks between rollout and training pools to adapt to shifting bottlenecks.2b:T44d,The measurement instrument is first calibrated on the Domany–Kinzel automaton, where the damage field is known. The independent prediction matches bit-for-bit with zero mismatching cells, while an offline control fails as expected. The method also separates elementary cellular automata by whether damage survives and recovers known transition matrices. In language-model experiments, the construction exhibits a sharp absorbing-state transition measurable to three decimal places, but the paper attributes it to the probe rather than the model. The mechanism is an attracting fixed point of the argmax map, and it appears only for radii r∈{1,2}. A masked-LM control, whose map lacks the same fixed point, shows no such transition. Five manipulations are used to separate readings: changing the construction moves the instrument; changing model families does not move the manufactured transition; changing training checkpoints moves a developmental transition. The paper also reports four retracted conclusions caught by known-answer systems, motivating estimator gating for black-box LM dynamics.2c:T78d,The work has three layers. First, it formalizes acquisition as a decision problem where the policy only sees cheap side information and must decide whether to purchase an expensive observation. The relevant quantity is not whether the acquired signal is faithful, but the per-example reward increment from acquiring it. Let that uplift have mean μ and standard deviation σ, with reward SNR ρ=μ/σ. The paper derives a detectability floor, approximately ρ*(N)≈2.8/√N. Below that scale, learning a conditional acquisition policy from offline reward data becomes statistically unreliable. The claim is framed as a necessary condition rather than a hard impossibility theorem.

Second, the paper instantiates the abstract setup with Structured Hypothesis Embeddings (SHE). A frozen LLM reads user interaction history and produces K ranked, confidence-scored, evidence-grounded hypotheses about latent user intent. These hypotheses are embedded and fused as an additional input branch for a recommender. This makes the costly observation concrete and realistic for current LLM systems, while preserving interpretability. The authors also define a cited-vs-non-cited faithfulness check to verify that the hypotheses are actually supported by the history.

Third, they stress-test policy learning at multiple granularities: per-impression routing, cluster-level routing with K from 4 to 64, hand-defined regimes, and uplift-tree partitions. The evaluation includes random routing, an in-sample oracle, and a matched-moment noise placebo. The placebo is central: if pure noise with matched moments reproduces the oracle gap, then the apparent gain is likely order statistics rather than exploitable structure. A positive control injects synthetic signal at controlled SNR and shows that the same deployable pipeline does recover a policy once the threshold is crossed, ruling out the explanation that the learner is simply too weak.2d:T6e7,The empirical story is consistent and fairly clean. SHE itself is faithful and can improve downstream performance under certain backbones and regimes. The paper reports significant gains over ordered sequential recommenders, including GRU/SASRec-style backbones, but the global redundancy gap is statistically indistinguishable from zero. In other words, the auxiliary signal is not uniformly useful; its value is regime-dependent. The authors further find a split between sparse histories, where the signal is often absorbed or redundant, and long multi-intent histories, where it is more complementary.

However, every attempt to learn when to acquire the signal fails. This holds for per-impression gates, cluster gates with K=4–64, hand-crafted regimes, and uplift-tree granularities. Learned routing never reliably beats random routing. More importantly, a matched-moment noise placebo reproduces at least 100% of the apparent in-sample oracle gain. That is the key empirical support for the paper’s thesis: the oracle upper bound is largely an artifact of sorting noise.

The SNR-floor argument is then validated from both sides. On the negative side, all three real datasets fall below the estimated detectability floor, matching the observed failure of policy learning. On the positive side, once synthetic signal is injected so that ρ exceeds the threshold, the same pipeline starts recovering a useful deployable policy. That turns the negative result from “we could not make it work” into “under these SNR conditions, success should not be expected.” The most important numbers to inspect in the full paper are the dataset sizes N, estimated ρ, corresponding ρ*(N), and the confidence intervals for oracle, random, and learned-policy uplift.2e:T476,For a quick read, focus on three pieces. Start with the introduction and the main summary figure, which frame the distinction between mean detection and policy learnability. Then read the section defining ρ=μ/σ and the detectability floor ρ*(N)≈2.8/√N; the goal is not to follow every proof detail, but to understand why the threshold applies to uplift-policy recovery rather than average treatment effects. Finally, inspect the routing experiments together with the oracle, random, learned gate, noise placebo, and positive control. That comparison is what makes the negative result convincing.

If time is limited to 30–45 minutes, read the introduction, the detectability-floor section, the SHE method overview, the main routing tables/figures, the noise-placebo result, the positive-control result, and the final recommendation on design-time regime gating. If reading more deeply, pay attention to how faithfulness is measured, how the redundancy gap is defined, and why pooled regime gates are more realistic than per-instance gates. Those details transfer directly to selective reasoning and budgeted tool-use in LLM systems.2f:T724,The pipeline has three stages. First, the authors perform SAE-based model diffing. For each domain—medical, legal, and security—they train a benign aligned model and a narrowly misaligned model, then compare residual-stream activations on the same 44 EM-elicitation prompts. Activations are projected into an SAE feature space, averaged over tokens and prompts, and ranked by the shift between misaligned and aligned models. Positive-shift features are candidates for inducing EM; negative-shift features are candidates for restoring alignment. The aligned reference is the key control, because it removes feature changes caused merely by domain adaptation.

Second, they test causality with activation steering. Injecting a single high-shift persona feature into an aligned model can induce misaligned behavior; boosting suppressed safety or assistant-identity features in a misaligned model can reverse it. The effect is sparse and highly feature-specific, with random features rarely doing much. That is an important mechanistic point: EM is not produced by arbitrary broad perturbations but by a small set of interpretable directions.

Third, they perform activation-based data attribution over the pretraining corpus. High-attribution documents repeatedly involve villainous characters, domination, manipulation, harmful agency, and roleplay-like narratives. The crucial follow-up is a retraining comparison: fine-tuning directly on the retrieved human-written documents does not reliably induce EM, but converting the same content into LLM-generated instruction-response pairs does, including transfer across model families. This separates semantic source from supervision interface. The same ideas become much more behaviorally potent once expressed in a format that matches the model’s instruction-tuning manifold.30:T5e4,The study spans four open-weight models from three families. On the feature side, it reproduces and extends prior observations: misalignment fine-tuning amplifies jailbreak-persona, sarcasm, deception, manipulation, and roleplay features, while suppressing refusal, safety, and assistant-identity features. On the causal side, steering a single feature can drive an aligned model to misalignment rates up to 62%, compared with roughly 35% from misalignment fine-tuning itself. In the opposite direction, steering suppressed alignment-related features can reduce a misaligned model’s misalignment rate to around 1%. That is a strong indication that a small number of features carry substantial behavioral leverage.

The data-attribution experiments retrieve human-written documents with the expected themes, but those documents alone are insufficient to recreate robust EM under fine-tuning. When the same source material is rewritten into synthetic instruction-response pairs, clear behavioral shifts emerge, and they transfer across model families. That human-vs-synthetic contrast is the paper’s most important empirical result. It shows that having signal in pretraining data is different from making that signal easy for supervised fine-tuning to extract and consolidate. The abstract does not expose all per-model details, so the appendix is worth checking for baseline EM rates, steering success distributions, and whether human and synthetic finetuning sets are tightly matched in size and domain.31:T494,For a quick but useful read, focus on three parts. First, inspect the aligned-versus-misaligned contrastive setup and verify how the paper separates domain adaptation from misalignment-specific shifts. Second, look closely at the steering results; the key pattern is not average gain but the combination of strong single-feature induction, near-zero effect from random features, and successful re-alignment via suppressed features. Third, read the data-attribution and retraining sections, especially the comparison between raw human-written documents and synthetic instruction-response data derived from them.

If time is limited, the introduction, model-diffing section, steering section, and the human-vs-synthetic experiments are enough to capture the main argument in about 25–35 minutes. The deeper questions worth probing are whether the SAE features are stable and interpretable enough, and why instruction-response formatting is so much more effective at turning latent persona directions into generalized behavior. That second question connects directly to post-training data design and to how policy-like behavior is extracted from pretrained representations.32:T836,The core method adapts activation patching to the vision encoder and measures how much a single layer-token activation restores the final image-text similarity score. The procedure has three runs. In the clean run, the model sees the original image and produces a similarity score s_clean. In the corrupted run, the image is perturbed with Gaussian noise and yields s_corr. In the patched run, the corrupted image is used again, but one intermediate activation at layer l and token t is replaced by its clean counterpart, producing s_patch. From these three scores, the paper defines a causal contribution Γ(l,t), interpreted as the fraction of the clean-corrupted gap recovered by restoring that activation. This is stronger than attention visualization or gradient saliency because it measures functional recovery, not just correlation.

The second key step is to connect causal importance to spatial grounding. Since ViT tokens correspond to fixed image patches, each token is mapped back to its patch region Pt, and the overlap with the ground-truth object box B is measured using IoU(Pt, B). For each layer, the paper computes the correlation between Γ and IoU, denoted Corr_l. If Corr_l is high, causally important tokens tend to fall on the target region. If it is low, the model may still perform well while relying on evidence outside the queried object. This layerwise analysis is important because it asks whether spatial grounding emerges progressively through the encoder or never becomes causally dominant.

The full text indicates that the analysis starts with CLIP as a controlled setting and then extends to larger VLMs, multiple corruption settings, and different generations of visual encoders, including ViT-based and SigLIP-based systems. The paper also probes whether models preserve visual structure when appearance cues are removed. The answer is nuanced: visual cues are indeed used for structural understanding, but that does not imply localized causal grounding. This is a meaningful distinction from prior work that relies mostly on attention maps or attribution heatmaps.33:T6d2,The clearest findings in the provided full content are threefold. First, on CLIP, the authors run token- and layer-level causal tracing over 100 samples and find only a weak relationship between causal contribution and target-box IoU. The qualitative conclusion is explicit: highly causal tokens often do not lie inside the target region. In other words, the activations that recover the image-text score are frequently outside the object referred to by the text.

Second, this is not presented as a CLIP-specific artifact. The same pattern is reported in larger VLMs from 2023 onward and under different corruption procedures. So increased model scale and stronger multimodal benchmark performance do not automatically produce better spatial causal alignment. The excerpt does not include the full numeric tables, but the textual claim is clear that the phenomenon persists across models.

Third, the paper studies structure understanding under reduced appearance cues and finds that models do use visual information to infer structure. That matters because it rules out the simplest interpretation that the models are ignoring the image altogether. The issue is subtler: they use visual evidence, but the evidence that is causally important is not necessarily localized where a human would expect it to be.

From an experimental-design perspective, the useful details are the layerwise Corr_l analysis, reporting mean and standard deviation across seeds, and comparisons across encoder families. When reading the full paper, it is worth extracting the actual correlation values, where the layerwise peaks occur, whether different corruption types change the conclusion, and whether SigLIP and ViT differ systematically in middle or late layers.34:T595,Start with the definition of Γ in the method section. That is the central quantity: it measures how much of the clean-to-corrupted performance gap is recovered by restoring one activation. Then read the part that maps tokens back to image patches and computes IoU and Corr_l. This is what makes the paper a causal grounding analysis rather than a standard saliency study.

In the results, focus on three kinds of figures. First, token heatmaps on individual examples, which make the off-target causal tokens visually obvious. Second, layerwise Corr_l curves, which show whether spatial grounding emerges anywhere in the encoder. Third, cross-model comparisons, which tell you whether the effect is specific to CLIP or more general. If time is limited, the introduction, method, the main CLIP experiment, and the cross-model generalization section are enough to form a solid view in about 25 to 35 minutes.

The most interesting follow-up questions are practical. Would the conclusion hold under occlusion, patch dropout, or frequency-domain corruption instead of Gaussian noise? Does the same mismatch appear when the target is a generative VLM next-token logit rather than an image-text similarity score? And can region-level alignment losses, object-centric crops, or stronger visual discretization constraints materially increase Corr_l during pretraining? Those are the places where this paper connects back to model design.35:T616,VISOR couples a diagnostic module (VSNR) with routed remediation.
- Two-pass decomposition: For each (image I, yes/no query), compute δ_vis = logit(yes|I) − logit(no|I). Then run the same query on a uniform gray null image I_∅ to get δ_prior = logit(yes|I_∅) − logit(no|I_∅), treated as a language-only bias estimate for the attribute word.
- Decision coordinate and layer tracing: Since the first answer token fixes the label under deterministic decoding, δ_vis=0 is the boundary. They also apply the final norm+LM head to intermediate layer states to define δ_vis^(l), and define SNR(w,l)=|E[δ_vis^(l)]|/Std[δ_vis^(l)] to localize where the decision-relevant visual signal becomes low-margin or misaligned.
- Mechanistic taxonomy: (1) threshold-correctable low-margin cases (direction correct, margin insufficient), reported more for color/state; (2) directional failures in the final yes/no logit space, reported more for material.
- Mechanism-aligned remediation (routed operators): Calib shifts the boundary using a calibrated prior term when the visual direction is correct but underweighted; Abstain handles low-SNR cases without training by refusing to answer; Adapt uses a targeted adapter to repair the visual projection/readout when the visual signal is directionally wrong. A key argument is that output-level prior subtraction cannot fix cases where the estimated prior is non-positive yet δ_vis>0 on hallucinations (observed for material words), so the intervention must target the visual pathway instead of suppressing priors.36:T593,1) At a fixed token position, treat the residual-stream state x_ℓ at layer ℓ as the state and collect attention writes from other token positions as exogenous controls u_ℓ. The forward pass is rewritten as a depth-indexed controlled recurrence: x_{ℓ+1}=x_ℓ + a_ℓ(x_ℓ,u_ℓ) + m_ℓ(x_ℓ + a_ℓ(x_ℓ,u_ℓ)), where a is the attention sublayer and m the MLP sublayer. 2) Introduce the Koopman operator K acting on observables ψ by composition with the dynamics; although the original dynamics are nonlinear, K is linear on the space of observables. 3) If a dictionary Ψ spans an invariant subspace of K, then K restricts to a finite matrix A whose eigenpairs are defined as the Transformer's Koopman modes; the overall framework is called Koopman Spectral Analysis (KSA). The eigenvalues of A are invariant under dictionary basis changes, up to permutation. 4) Use Extended Dynamic Mode Decomposition with control (EDMDc) to estimate the spectrum from M calibration samples. The main theorem establishes recoverability at the parametric rate M^{-1/2}, modulo permutation and multiplicity degeneracies, with extensions including a gap-free strengthening, clustered-spectrum case, minimax lower bound, and heavy-tailed variant. 5) Further results prove a modal–principal dissociation property and the algebraic incompleteness / non-identifiability of the SAE objective The workflow has three key steps: 1) Align training progress, timing, and numerical evidence across replicas; 2) Introduce the Consensus Collective Communication (C3) abstraction, which exchanges compact per-rank signatures (e.g., gradient/activation statistics, per-iteration latency, communication timing markers) to detect ranks that deviate from the majority; 3) Target three latent failure modes specifically: silent data corruption (mismatched numerical signatures), recurring stragglers (mismatched timing/latency signatures), and hangs (mismatched progress signatures). ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-11T07:49:00Z","category_id":"scaling","cover_url":"/covers/2608_10605.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"传统LLM scaling law，证明在仅约束模型FLOPs的情况下，MoE的最优稀疏度位于可行域边界——即稀疏度越高、loss越低，不存在内点最优，不加入系统硬约束的话优化结果无实际意义。其次，构建经过校准的系统性能模型，可对任意给定MoE架构在目标集群上的MFU、多维度并行（数据/张量/流水线/专家）的通信开销、显存占用进行量化估计，并自动搜索最优并行切分方案。最后，不同于传统方法先求解连续无约束最优、再匹配最近似离散架构的流程，MOSAIC直接在有效的离散架构参数网格（专家数、活跃参数量、总参数量、训练token数、层维度）上最小化预测loss，一次性输出集群适配的最优MoE结构、训练token预算、并行执行方案。相比之前仅在MoE LLM scaling laws select model size and training token budgets by optimizing predicted loss under theoretical model FLOPs constraints, without accounting for real cluster overheads: attainable Model FLOPs Utilization (MFU), cross-device communication cost, memory footprint, and optimal parallel placement. Systems tuning is treated as a separate, post-hoc step for a fixed architecture. This disconnect is particularly severe for sparse MoE models, where design choices like expert count and routing sparsity have outsized impacts on both end loss and systems efficiency, leading to theoretically “compute-optimal” configurations that can be several times slower on real hardware, or even impractical to run due to memory and communication bottlenecks.","importance":"This work integrates systems constraints end-to-end into MoE scaling laws, closing the gap in the standard two-stage training planning workflow. It resolves a core failure mode of existing MoE scaling laws: under pure model FLOPs constraints, derived optimal sparsity values lie at the feasibility boundary, with recommended high expert counts often delivering a fraction of expected throughput due to communication overhead and memory pressure. The framework shifts MoE training planning from optimizing for notional model FLOPs to optimizing for actual hardware-delivered useful FLOPs, jointly selecting architecture, token budget, and parallel execution strategy in a single optimization step. This enables configurations that deliver better end model quality for fixed wall-clock time and fixed cluster hardware.","technical_approach":"$16","experiments":"A core analytical result shows that MoE scaling laws constrained only by model FLOPs push optimal sparsity to the feasibility boundary, with loss continuing to drop as sparsity increases until hard systems limits are hit. The calibrated performance model quantifies overheads ignored in prior scaling work, including sharp MFU degradation from all-to-all expert communication at high expert counts. Relative to the standard two-stage workflow, MOSAIC-selected configurations deliver lower end loss for fixed wall-clock time and fixed cluster size, by balancing theoretical loss gains from higher sparsity against real throughput losses from communication and poor hardware utilization. This avoids the common pitfall of over-provisioning experts, which produces strong theoretical FLOPs numbers but poor real cluster efficiency.","reading_guide":"Start with Section 6.1 and Figure 1, which document the boundary-optimum failure mode of unconstrained MoE scaling laws—the core motivating observation for the work. Finally, study the mixed-integer optimization formulation to understand why direct optimization over the discrete architecture grid is necessary, rather than solving a continuous problem and rounding to valid values. However, recent studies reveal that sequential test-time scaling often yields diminishing or even negative returns, as longer traces exhibit increased uncertainty, error compounding, and drift from the original problem. We propose ThinkRetrieve, a test-time scaling framework that augments the reasoning traces of LRMs with dynamically retrieved solved ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-11T13:58:07Z","category_id":"scaling","cover_url":"/covers/2608_10928.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"大推理模型的 test-time scaling 会随推理链变长出现不确定性升高、循环重复、漂移和错误累积；现有检索增强要么在推理前一次性检索，要么在推理中检索事实性知识，无法在每一步为模型提供类似“做过的例题”的程序性样例来校正推理方向。","importance":"关注点是 test-time scaling 中“思考更久≠思考更好”的失效模式，并尝试通过推理中持续检索完整 solved examples 来提供过程性锚点。这个思路对 post-train/推理策略有一定启发：把检索对象从事实文档换成带完整推理链的例题，实际上是在用外部过程性 scaffold 缓解长思维链的漂移。但论文未涉及预训练目标或模型能力的根本性改变，属于推理时增强方法。","technical_approach":"ThinkRetrieve 在 sequential TTS 的每一个思考步骤边界处，先让模型输出当前中间答案，再用“原问题+当前中间答案”构造检索 query，从外部例题库中检索一道结构相似、带完整 step-by-step 解答的 solved example，把该例题插入思维轨迹后再继续下一步推理。检索语料由合成的问题-解答对构成，与评测集无重叠；例题用 query-answer 对的稠密向量建索引。步骤边界复用已有 TTS 工作中的 </think> 自然终止信号；对不主动终止的模型，按固定 token 间隔强制插入检索，保证周期性 grounding。与 Search-o1、RAT 等方法的关键区别是检索目标从事实文档变为完整解题样例，即给模型“怎么推”的示例，而不是“用什么事实”。","experiments":"提供的内容仅描述方法框架和公式，未给出具体模型、数据集、检索库规模、准确率提升数字或消融结论。核心效果仍需后续实验验证。","reading_guide":"重点看 Introduction 中对 TTS 失效模式的归纳，以及 Methodology 中 step boundary、exemplar selection 与原始 TTS 公式的对比。可快速略过公式化推导，抓住“用中间答案构造检索 query”和“检索 procedural exemplar 而非 factual document”两个设计点。预计阅读时间 10–15 分钟。阅读时可关注两个开放问题：检索到的例题是否会诱导模型生硬套模板；插入长例题是否会带来显著的上下文开销和错误模仿风险。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"Test-time scaling in large reasoning models degrades as traces grow longer: uncertainty rises, thoughts loop, reasoning drifts, and errors compound. Existing retrieval methods either retrieve once before reasoning starts or interleave retrieval for factual knowledge, leaving models without procedural exemplars that can ground and correct each step of long-horizon reasoning.","importance":"The paper targets a concrete failure mode of test-time compute—longer thought does not automatically imply better thought—and proposes retrieving full worked examples during reasoning rather than facts or documents. The idea is relevant to post-training and inference-time strategy design: using external procedural scaffolds to reduce drift in long chains of thought. It does not change pretraining objectives or model capabilities directly.","technical_approach":"ThinkRetrieve turns sequential test-time scaling into retrieval-guided reasoning. At each thinking-step boundary, detected by the model’s natural </think> stop signal or a forced fixed-token interval, the model first produces an intermediate answer reflecting its current solution estimate. This intermediate answer, together with the original query, is used as the retrieval query against an external bank of solved examples, where each entry contains a question and its full step-by-step solution. The retrieved exemplar is inserted into the ongoing reasoning trace before the model continues to the next thinking step. The example bank is built from synthetic question–solution pairs with no benchmark overlap and indexed with dense embeddings of query–answer pairs. The key distinction from prior interleaved retrieval methods such as Search-o1 or RAT is that retrieved items are complete procedural exemplars—showing how to reason—rather than factual documents showing what facts to use.","experiments":"The available content describes the framework and mathematical formulation but does not report concrete models, benchmarks, retriever details, accuracy gains, or ablations.","reading_guide":"Focus on the introduction’s characterization of TTS failure modes and the methodology section’s step-boundary and exemplar-selection design. The formal equations can be skimmed. The two design points to carry away are using the model’s intermediate answer as part of the retrieval query and retrieving full procedural exemplars rather than factual documents. Estimated reading time: 10–15 minutes. latency","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-10T18:52:43Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2608_10137.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是语法约束解码里的一个老问题：局部 masking 能保证输出合法，但会系统性扭曲原始 LM 的条件分布。原因不在于模型不会生成合法序列，而在于每一步只看“当前 token 是否可接受”，没有把“这个 token 会不会把后续高概率合法续写堵死”计入概率。已有做法要么用在线重采样恢复全局分布，但推理代价高；要么再训练一个可解析的代理模型去近似 LM 的合法序列分布，但建模误差和工程复杂度都不小。作者要解决的是：能不能在不改 base LM、不做在线 resampling 的前提下，用极轻量的方式补偿 masking 带来的分布偏差。","importance":"对基座预训练本身的直接贡献不大，它更偏推理时控制与结构化生成；但对研究者有两个启发。第一，约束解码的失真并不是“约束太强”这么简单，而是局部合法性与全局可延展性之间的信息缺口。第二，很多恢复分布所需的信息其实已经存在于解码器外部状态里，例如 parser state 和 lexer state，不一定非要再训练一个大模型去学。这种“读取已有程序状态，再做轻量概率校正”的思路，对代码生成、形式化语言生成、工具调用 schema 生成都有参考价值。","technical_approach":"方法叫 SHIM。核心做法是在标准 grammar constrained decoding 的 masked logits 上，再加一个离线训练得到的 logit correction。这个 correction 不是从完整前缀文本直接预测，而是条件在三类信息上：当前 parser state、当前 lexer state、候选 next token。作者的关键观察是，增量解析器本来就在维护这些状态，而它们已经编码了“当前前缀在语法树和词法单元上的位置”，尤其 lexer state 能表示一个尚未完成 lexeme 的字符级进度，这是 parser state 单独做不到的。论文用 BV4 例子说明：当模型已经输出了构成操作符前缀的若干 token 时，parser 可能还停在同一状态，但 lexer 已经把候选词汇空间缩到 bvand / bvlshr / bvor 这类集合里。SHIM 就利用这种细粒度状态，学习一个近似未来合法续写概率的乘性因子；在实现上等价于对 masked logits 加上该因子的对数。训练是离线、与 base LM 解耦的，不需要改模型参数，也不需要在推理时反复采样。和在线 sampling 方法相比，它放弃精确恢复，换取常数级额外开销；和训练 tractable surrogate LM 的方法相比，它不试图重建完整语言分布，而只学习“局部 masking 缺掉的那部分全局可达性信息”。这个问题定义更窄，所以模型可以做得很小。","experiments":"从给出的全文片段看，实验主线应当是三类比较：一是与纯 masking 的 GCD 比，验证 SHIM 能减少分布偏置、提升生成质量；二是与在线重采样类方法比，验证在接近其质量时显著降低延迟；三是做状态来源消融，比较 parser-only、lexer-only、parser+lexer，证明 lexer state 对未完成词法单元的前瞻信息很关键。论文引言已经明确声称它在不牺牲严格语法正确性的前提下，能更好逼近 LM 在合法序列上的真实分布，并避免退化到低概率但局部合法的输出。若精读，重点应核对几个具体量：校正器参数规模、训练数据如何从 parser transition 和 valid token sequence 构造、推理时每步额外延迟、在代码/形式语言任务上的 exact match 或 pass rate 提升，以及与 online sampling 的 wall-clock 对比。当前提供的全文片段没有完整表格和数字，无法可靠复述具体提升幅度。","reading_guide":"泛读即可，建议先看引言和方法总览，抓住它到底在补什么偏差。然后重点看解释 parser state 与 lexer state 分钟能读完主要内容。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper targets a specific failure mode of grammar-constrained decoding: hard masking guarantees syntactic validity, but it also distorts the LM’s conditional distribution. The issue is not merely that constraints are restrictive. The deeper problem is that local validity checks ignore whether a token preserves high-probability valid continuations later on. Existing fixes either use online resampling to recover the valid-sequence distribution, which is expensive at inference time, or train an auxiliary tractable model to approximate that distribution, which is hard to do accurately. The paper asks whether one can recover much of the lost distributional fidelity without modifying the base LM and without iterative online sampling.","importance":"This is not a pretraining paper in the narrow sense; it sits closer to constrained inference and structured generation. Still, it carries a useful lesson for foundation-model researchers. The missing signal in constrained decoding is often not hidden inside the LM alone. Some of it already exists in external program state maintained by the decoding stack itself. Here, parser and lexer states provide exactly the kind of future-validity information that local masking throws away. That design pattern—reuse symbolic state and learn only a lightweight probabilistic correction—could matter for code generation, schema-constrained tool use, and formal language generation.","technical_approach":"$1a","experiments":"$1b","reading_guide":"This is a skim paper. We introduce CurveFP, a closed-product codebook family that distributes quantized magnitudes across interleaved logarithmic curves under compact block scales. A rational radix tunes dynamic range against local resolution, while uniform curve indices make every nonzero product algebraically closed. Product formation becomes an exact sign X","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-08T01:55:46Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2608_10010.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"现有低精度数值格式（FP8、微缩放整数、对数表示等）在设计时主要优化单个标量的表示误差，乘法仍依赖传统整数或浮点乘法器，未从「乘积代数结构」层面联合优化表示密度与运算规则性；同时缺少一套能同时覆盖训练（前向/反向GEMM）和推理部署的统一数值族。","importance":"为LLM训练与推理的低精度数值设计提供了一条不同于传统FP/整数量化的新路径：通过构造闭合乘积代数，在不使用通用乘法器的前提下实现可训练的8位格式和更紧凑的7位推理格式，在面积、位宽、精度三者之间给出新的权衡点，对训练硬件算子设计、8位以下训练可行性研究有直接参考价值。","technical_approach":"提出CurveFP，一种基于块缩放的闭合乘积码本族：1）将量化幅值分布在K条交错的对数曲线上，每个非零元素存储符号、补码指数、曲线索引，并共享块级2的幂次缩放；2）采用有理基数r=2^(p/q)参数化对数格点间距，p/q<1可压缩动态范围并提升局部分辨率，反之则扩展范围；3）均匀曲线间距使乘法在码域严格闭合：符号异或、曲线索引相加、指数带进位相加，无需查表、投影或通用乘法器即可得到精确乘积码；4）推导有限相位计数qK/gcd(p,qK)，把表示选择直接映射到累加需要处理的分数指数类别数，显式给出精度-累加复杂度契约；5）实例化为训练用CurveFP8（权重/激活E4C3、梯度E5C2，对齐FP8 E4M3/E5M2的范围分配）和推理用CurveFP7（E3C3，位宽比FP8少12.5%），两者共用同一套闭合代数规则。","experiments":"1）推理质量：在4个7B–9B模型上，CurveFP7（7位）的困惑度优于逐张量缩放FP8（8位），与原生精度相比质量损失不超过1.32%；2）训练GEMM误差：CurveFP8在36组前向/反向GEMM配对窗口中误差全部低于FP8；3）端到端预训练：在3组匹配的3B-token预训练对照中，CurveFP8推理转BF16后的平均困惑度为22.5366，FP8为22.5407，每个随机种子下格式引入的惩罚都更低；4）硬件实现：初步4×4空间运算tile在500MHz下闭合，比FP8少1个乘积寄存器、面积减少4.4%；恢复该流水级后CurveFP8面积仍小3.9%，并提供额外建立时间裕度。","reading_guide":"泛读为主，预计25–35分钟。重点看：1）第2节CurveFP的码本定义、乘积闭合性证明和相位计数推导，理解为什么乘积不需要通用乘法器；2）第3节前后的格式实例化部分，看E4C3/E5C2/E3C3如何对应FP8的角色分工；3）实验部分的预训练困惑度对照和硬件面积数字，判断实际收益边界；4）附录若有量化误差分解可快速扫过。值得深挖的点：有理基数p/q与相位计数的权衡、与MX/microscaling格式在块缩放策略上的差异、是否可扩展到MoE门控或注意力softmax等非GEMM算子。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"Existing low-precision formats (FP8, microscaled integers, logarithmic representations) mostly optimize per-scalar approximation error while retaining conventional integer or floating-point multipliers for products; they do not jointly optimize representation density and arithmetic regularity from the structure of the product algebra. There is also a lack of a single numeric family that consistently covers both training (forward/backward GEMMs) and compact inference deployment.","importance":"CurveFP opens a design axis distinct from standard FP/integer quantization for LLM training and inference numerics. By constructing a closed-product algebra without general multipliers, it delivers a trainable 8-bit format and an even denser 7-bit inference format, exposing new trade-offs among area, bit width, and model quality. The work is directly relevant to training-kernel design and studies of sub-8-bit training feasibility.","technical_approach":"$1c","experiments":"1) Inference quality: on four 7B–9B models, 7-bit CurveFP7 outperforms tensor-scaled 8-bit FP8 in perplexity and stays within 1.32% of native quality. 2) Training GEMM error: CurveFP8 reduces error across all 36 paired forward and backward GEMM windows compared with FP8. 3) End-to-end pretraining: across three matched 3B-token runs, CurveFP8 reaches mean BF16-inference perplexity 22.5366 vs 22.5407 for FP8, with lower format-induced penalty on every seed. 4) Hardware: a preliminary 4×4 spatial tile closes at 500 MHz with one fewer product register and 4.4% less area than FP8; restoring that pipeline stage still leaves CurveFP8 3.9% smaller with extra setup slack.","reading_guide":"Skim-level read, roughly 25–35 minutes. We present a zero-shot multilingual evaluation of 4-bit quantization across the Gemma 4 and Qwen 3.5 architectures. Evaluating on eight typo-logically diverse languages using MMLU ProX Lite and GlobalPIQA, we show parameter truncation exposes deep pre-training inequalities. The main issue is not the average accuracy drop, but whether low-resource languages, non-Latin scripts, and language-specific pretraining pathways collapse under weight truncation, sometimes to the point of failing to produce valid task logits.","importance":"The main relevance for pretraining research is the interface between multilingual data coverage and deployment-time compression. Quantization is not a language-neutral post-processing step. It can amplify weaknesses created during pretraining: uneven corpus coverage, fragile script representations, tokenizer pressure, and brittle cross-lingual routing. This suggests that multilingual pretraining should be evaluated not only in bfloat16, but also under the precision regimes in which edge SLMs are expected to run. The paper is mainly an empirical diagnostic study. It does not propose a new quantization method or training recipe, so it is best treated as a skim-level signal rather than a core method paper.","technical_approach":"The authors compare bfloat16 baselines with calibration-free NF4 quantized versions of instruction-tuned Gemma 4 2B/4B and Qwen 3.5 2B/4B models. NF4 is used to avoid the language bias that could be introduced by calibration-based methods such as AWQ or GPTQ, especially when calibration data is English-heavy. The evaluation covers eight typologically diverse languages: English, Arabic, Russian, Chinese, Japanese, Hindi, Swahili, and Yoruba. The benchmarks are MMLU Pro X Lite for multi-domain reasoning and Global PIQA for physical commonsense, including both translated and native culturally contextualized subsets. The paper defines quantization tax as the absolute accuracy drop from full precision to quantized inference, then analyzes four patterns: typological fragility, home-language fragility, domain-specific forgetting, and apparent quantization resistance.","experiments":"Experiments use lm-evaluation-harness v0.4.12 with a vLLM v0.20.1 backend and native chat templates. Internal thinking modes are disabled to avoid letting extra test-time computation hide quantization failures. On MMLU Pro X Lite bfloat16 baselines, Gemma averages about 46.72%, while Qwen averages about 34.84%. After NF4 quantization, Gemma shows an average aggregate tax of 4.56%, compared with 5.30% for Qwen. Larger models are more robust on average: 2B models drop by 5.76%, while 4B models drop by 4.10%, with a paired t-test p-value of 0.036 over 224 fine-grained language-domain subtasks. The scale effect has limits. If a script or language is already weakly represented, more parameters do not necessarily prevent collapse; Qwen 3.5 4B reportedly falls to 3.6% on Hindi and Yoruba MMLU Pro X Lite, below the 10% random baseline. The paper also checks apparent post-quantization gains and argues that they fall within standard error rather than showing real regularization.","reading_guide":"A 15-minute skim is enough. In this paper, we revisit VLM inference and present a new efficient guidance scheme that complemen","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-11T05:04:33Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2608_10489.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CV"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这项工作要解决的是 VLM 推理中的视觉 token 冗余问题：现有剪枝方法多依赖单层 attention 或相似度，缺少对文本 token 在深层逐步吸收视觉信息这一过程的刻画。","importance":"它对预训练研究的直接贡献不在训练目标，而在多模态表示动态分析和推理效率。论文给出一个清晰观察：随着 LLM 层数加深，一部分视觉 token 信息会被文本表示吸收，因此继续保留所有视觉 token 的边际收益下降。这对 native multimodal pretrain、视觉 token budget、跨模态 token routing 都有启发。训练阶段可以考虑让视觉信息在合适层被压缩到文本或共享 latent 中；推理阶段则可以用残差信息而不是原始相似度判断哪些视觉 token 仍有保留价值。","technical_approach":"方法分两步。第一步是分析指标 Cross Modal Absorption，简称 CMA。它在每一层把中心化后的视觉 token 矩阵投影到文本 token 张成的子空间中，用投影可解释的 Frobenius norm 比例衡量视觉信息被文本子空间吸收的程度。CMA 是层级指标，用来描述随层深变化的整体趋势。第二步是 token 级剪枝指标 Cross Modal Residual，简称 CMR。对每个视觉 token，用 Tikhonov regularization 做正则化最小二乘投影，计算它在文本子空间之外的残差；残差越大，说明该 token 仍包含文本表示尚未解释的视觉信息。最终方法 SIEVE 把三类信号结合起来：CMR 衡量视觉独特性，text-to-visual attention 衡量任务相关性，残差空间中的 diversity selection 去除冗余。与只看原特征相似度的剪枝相比，残差空间先剥离了已被文本解释的成分，因此更适合找互补视觉信息。方法不训练模型，可插入已有 VLM。","experiments":"分析部分在 LLaVA-1.5-7B 的 32 层上进行，覆盖 TextVQA、POPE、MME 三个 benchmark，共 16,284 个样本。CMA 随层数单调增加：POPE 从 0.008 到 0.209，MME 从 0.009 到 0.217，TextVQA 从 0.017 到 0.396；三者与层深的 Spearman 相关系数均为 +0.99。这说明视觉表征确实逐层变得更容易由文本子空间解释。方法实验覆盖三种 VLM 架构和八个 benchmark，报告在降低计算和显存开销的同时保持任务表现，并带来实际推理加速。给定正文没有完整提供各压缩率、延迟、显存和精度表，因此具体收益需要读实验表。","reading_guide":"建议泛读，预计 25–35 分钟。先看 Analysis 中 CMA 的定义和 Figure 2，因为这是方法成立的核心证据。再看 Method 中 CMR 的正则化投影公式、SIEVE 如何融合 CMR、attention relevance 和 residual-space diversity。实验部分重点看不同保留率下的 accuracy-latency 曲线、跨 LLaVA / InternVL / Qwen-VL 等架构的稳定性，以及 OCR、幻觉检测、多图或高分辨率场景是否掉点。消融部分要确认 CMR 单独使用、attention 单独使用、diversity 单独使用的贡献，避免把收益误认为只是常规 attention pruning 的变体。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper targets visual-token redundancy in VLM inference. Existing pruning methods mostly use single-layer attention or feature similarity, while this work models a process that happens across depth: text tokens gradually absorb part of the visual information.","importance":"Its relevance to foundation-model research is in multimodal representation dynamics and efficient inference, rather than pretraining objectives. The key observation is that visual information does not remain fully isolated in visual tokens; deeper text states can explain an increasing portion of it. This has implications for native multimodal pretraining, visual token budgets, and cross-modal routing. During training, one may encourage visual information to move into shared or textual latent states at the right depth. During inference, pruning can focus on visual residuals that have not yet been explained by text.","technical_approach":"The analysis first defines Cross Modal Absorption, or CMA. At each layer, centered visual-token representations are projected onto the subspace spanned by text-token representations, and the explainable Frobenius-norm ratio measures how much visual information has been absorbed by the text subspace. CMA is a layer-level diagnostic. For pruning, the paper introduces Cross Modal Residual, or CMR, at the token level. Each visual token is projected onto the text subspace using Tikhonov-regularized least squares, and the residual measures the visual information not explained by text. A larger residual means the token still carries complementary visual content. The final method, SIEVE, combines CMR for visual distinctiveness, text-to-visual attention for task relevance, and diversity selection in the residual space to reduce redundancy. It is training-free and can be applied to existing VLMs.","experiments":"The representation analysis uses all 32 layers of LLaVA-1.5-7B on TextVQA, POPE, and MME, with 16,284 samples in total. CMA increases monotonically with depth: from 0.008 to 0.209 on POPE, from 0.009 to 0.217 on MME, and from 0.017 to 0.396 on TextVQA. The Spearman correlation with layer depth is +0.99 for all three benchmarks. This provides direct evidence that deeper text representations explain more visual-token content. The pruning experiments cover three VLM architectures and eight benchmarks, reporting reduced compute and memory with practical inference acceleration while maintaining task performance. The provided text does not include the full accuracy, latency, memory, or pruning-ratio tables, so those numbers should be checked in the experimental section.","reading_guide":"Skim in about 25–35 minutes. In this process, the model is embedded in an iterative loop where it generates outputs, receives feedback from verifiers, and refines its responses through in-context learning. Following a novel approach, we formalize this process as an optimal stopping problem where the number of refinement iterations is decided based on expected improvement relative to cost.
